Qatar & Middle East — Daily·Weekly Media Monitor

Twice a day (07:00 and 15:30 Qatar time), major coverage from Qatari, Iranian, other major countries' and Korean outlets is compiled into AI issue summaries with source links. A list of the latest reports from relevant institutions is also available for reference lower on the page, and every Sunday a weekly report recapping the past week is published. [Commercial Section, Embassy of the Republic of Korea in Qatar · KOTRA Doha]

🧭 Key issue briefs AI summary
Issue 1

⚔️ Houthi Airstrikes on Saudi Arabia and Occupation of Red Sea Island — Escalation of Yemen Civil War and Saudi-US Security Cooperation

Key summary
  • Houthi rebels claimed to have crossed the Saudi border to strike an air base and announced the additional occupation of a strategic Red Sea island, while Yemeni government forces repelled a Houthi offensive in western Taiz and launched a counterattack. According to WHO figures, fighting across Yemen during the week of September 6–12 resulted in 504 deaths, 2,379 injuries, and approximately 78,000 displaced persons.
  • Saudi Arabia's East-West oil pipeline was destroyed in a drone attack, identified as a direct threat to Saudi Arabia's crude oil export routes.

Issue 2

🛢️ International Oil Prices Surge (Brent Breaks $100) — Fallout from Supply Disruptions at Hormuz and in Saudi Arabia

Key summary
  • Following the attack on Saudi Arabia's East-West pipeline and heightened tensions in the Strait of Hormuz, Brent crude surged to the $107–108 per barrel range (up more than $4 intraday); the United States stated that transit through Hormuz was 'being normalized,' but the market reflected the break above $100 in oil prices.

Issue 3

🚢 GCC-Iran Talks on Hormuz Postponed — Iran Expands Sanctioned Vessel List to 77 Ships

Key summary
  • GCC-Iran talks on Hormuz Strait navigation, scheduled to be held in Muscat, Oman, were postponed at Saudi Arabia's request, with Iran citing 'developments in the Yemen situation' as the reason for the delay, stalling progress on an interim Hormuz solution. Saudi Arabia is understood to be reluctant to engage in dialogue with Tehran due to anger over the Houthi airstrikes on Saudi Arabia and the occupation of the Red Sea island.

This page automatically collects and summarizes public news coverage and material from relevant institutions across Qatar, Iran, other major countries and Korea. News is gathered by querying Google News RSS feeds, Naver News search, and outlet RSS (Al Jazeera, BBC, etc.) with keywords such as Qatar, the Middle East and energy; in-depth reports are collected directly from the publication boards and RSS of domestic and international research institutes, international organizations and consultancies (supplemented by news search targeting those publishers), keeping only analyses and reports on the Middle East situation, the Iran war and their industrial, supply-chain and energy repercussions. The report list accumulates items published since the war's outbreak (2026-02-28), split into domestic and overseas and ordered by publication date (newest first). Collection and summarization rely on each outlet's publicly available headline and snippet; for subscription or paywalled outlets, the full article can be opened through the provided source link only after the reader signs in or subscribes to that outlet directly. The collected articles are automatically clustered and summarized by issue, and translated into English and Arabic, by Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 5) AI. The whole process runs and publishes automatically twice a day (07:00 and 15:30 Qatar time) via GitHub Actions. The Qatar government activity section separately curates only official statements, announcements and actions on the war and regional situation by Qatar government and state entities — the Emir, the Prime Minister, the ministries of Foreign Affairs, Defense and Interior, the Government Communications Office (GCO), QatarEnergy and the state news agency (QNA). Owing to automated collection some articles or reports may be missed, so please re-verify important issues against the original sources and further searches.
- Embassy of the Republic of Korea in Qatar, Commercial Section · KOTRA Doha
